March 1, 2022 
 
 
 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins 
City of Cheyenne 
2101 O’Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
RE: 
City of Cheyenne Retail Liquor License #31: 
Transfer from Dillinger’s, LLC 
To Prime Realty, LLC 
Dear Mayor Collins: 
Please be advised that the holder of Liquor License #31, Dillinger’s, LLC  can no longer utilize 
said license and wishes to transfer the referenced liquor license back to Prime Realty, LLC.   We are 
asking for approval of this transfer by the Governing Body.  As of March 11th, 2022 at 11.59 pm and up 
and until the transfer of license back to Prime Realty, LLC, the License will be “parked” at 1601 Central 
Ave. 
 
Immediately following the transfer back to Prime Realty, LLC, Prime Realty will be parking Liquor 
License #31 at 1601 Central Ave until the transfer to the new tenant can be processed and approved by 
the governing body.   
 
Prime Realty has found a tenant for the space who wishes to utilize the liquor license for their 
business operations and have applied for a transfer to that entity.  We thank the Mayor and City 
Council for their consideration of this request. 
 
Please find the enclosed Prime Realty, LLC application and requested fees for the transfer in 
conformance with all City of Cheyenne requirements thereto. 
 
If any further information is needed, please do not hesitate to contact me. 
 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
Dan Surdam 
Member, Prime Realty, LLC 

ASSIGNMENT OF LIQUOR LICENSE
THIS ASSIGNMENT by and between Dillinger’s
, LLC, the licensee of City of
Cheyenne LiquorLicense#3 1 (the “Assignor”), and Prime Realty
, LLC (the “Assignee”).
WITNESSETH
WHEREAS, Assignor is the holder of City of Cheyenne Liquor License #31 (the “Liquor
License”): and
WHEREAS, Assignor, as Tenant, and Assignee, as Landlord, are parties to that certain
Transfer Agreement affecting the Liquor License and that certain Lease Agreement dated
January
2019, regarding the Premises located at 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne,
Laramie County, Wyoming, a/k/a the Grier Building; and
WHEREAS, Assignee was the holder of the Liquor License and, on or about December
2019, as an element ofthe Lease Agreement, assigned the Liquor License to Assignor for
use by Assignor in the operation of its business under the Lease Agreement; and
WHEREAS, the Lease Agreement requires that, in the event of default by Assignor,
Assignor shall reassign the Liquor License to Assignee; and
WHEREAS, as of the date ofthe submission ofthis Assignment to the City Clerk of the
City of Cheyenne, Assignor is in default under the Lease Agreement and/or the Transfer
Agreement.
NOW, THEREFORE, by and for their mutual covenants herein contained and for value
received, the parties hereto agree as follows:
I. Assignor hereby assigns each and all of its rights, title and interest in City of
Cheyenne Liquor License #31 to Assignee, subject only to the approval ofthe Governing Body
ofthe City of Cheyenne.
2. Assignee hereby accepts the assignment of City of Cheyenne Liquor License #31,
subject only to the approval of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne.
Dated this
day of
14tr
, 20L.

RE:
City of Cheyenne Liquor License #31
Transfer from Prime Realty, LLC to Wyoming Horse Racing, LLC
Located at 1601 Central Avenue
We are requesting the City of Cheyenne allow us to transfer license #31 to Wyoming Horse Racing, LLC.
They will be located in the same building that the current License is assigned to, 1601 Central Aye,
— also
known as the Grier Bldg. This is an exciting time for downtown and we believe this transfer is consistent
with the long-term goals of the City and the Downtown Development Authority.
Please consider this
letter our official request that this transfer be made.
Sincerely,
— AuthenfisK

212812022 7:43:00 PM GMT
Dan Surdam
Member, Prime Realty, LLC

Wyoming Horse Racing, LLC
PD Box 1247
Cheyenne, WY 82003
Mayor Patrick Collins
City of Cheyenne
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
RE:
City of Cheyenne Liquor License #31
Transfer from Prime Realty, LLC to Wyoming Horse Racing, LLC
Located at 1601 Central Avenue
Dear Mayor Collins:
We have just signed a Lease for the 1st floor and mezzanine level of the Grier Building located at
1601 Central Ave. in historic downtown Cheyenne. Prime Realty, LLC currently holds a Liquor License for
that space which was formally Dillinger’s.
We are requesting a transfer of this license, #31, to our establishment to compliment the
operation of a Historic Horse Racing Off-Track Betting facility. We are excited about our new location
and look forward to assisting the Downtown establishments in their growth and viabUity.
We have completed the necessary application to facilitate this transfer. Please feel free to call
with any additional questions you may have regarding this request.
Sincerely,
Paul Ryder
General Manager, Wyoming Horse Racing, LLC

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-08_TotalExtraction_48062409.pdf
ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) TO CREATE A COMMON LANDSCAPING PLAN AND 
DEFINITION OF DEVELOPMENT FOOTPRINT.”  
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2. That the intent of this text amendment is to create a common landscaping plan 
and definition of development footprint. 
 
Section 3. That on February 22, 2022, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment with modifications. 
 
Section 4. That Section 1.4.3 of the UDC is amended to add the following definition:  
 
“Development Footprint. The development footprint is the total land area that is 
permanently affected by development activities, and includes buildings, structures, 
hardscape, utilities, roads and parking areas, maneuvering areas, storage areas, areas 
associated with the use on the property, and areas cleared of natural vegetation and 
topsoil.”  
 
Section 5. That Section 6.3.10, is created to read as follows:  
 
“6.3.10 Common Landscape Plan 
As an alternative to individual site landscaping, developments of eighty (80) acres or 
larger in Light Industrial (LI) and Heavy Industrial (HI) zoning districts may submit a 
common landscape plan that would contribute to uniformity of a development’s 
collective appearance. Common landscape plans shall focus on providing landscaping 
that is beneficial to the public, consistent with the general landscape character and 
context of the area, and water conservation. Common landscape plans shall be 
administratively approved by the Director. 
 
a. Submittal Requirements.  Applications for a common landscape plan may be 
initiated by all property owner(s) of all property subject to the common 
landscape plan, or an agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) 
through mutual agreement or a property owner’s association. 
 
b. Application, Submittal, and Phasing. An applicant shall submit a conceptual 
common landscape plan, prepared by a licensed landscape architect, for the 
entire development based on presumed development footprints for the plan 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:39 am, Mar 04, 2022

 
 
Page 2 of 4 
area. Installation of landscaping may be developed in phases and the 
developer shall submit phasing plan in conjunction with submittal of common 
landscape plan.  Phasing plans shall show general locations of landscaping, 
materials, plantings, groundcover, anticipated development footprints, and any 
transferred points within the plan area.  As development occurs, site / 
landscape plans shall be submitted and reviewed by the City to determine that 
each site meets the intent of the common landscape plan. 
 
c. Common Landscape Plan Standards.  The landscape standards of Chapter 
6.3 of the UDC shall apply, except as set forth below.  
 
1. Required Internal Landscape Area. The Required Internal Landscape 
Area of Section 6.3.3 shall be ten (10) percent of the development 
footprint. Required internal landscaping may be transferred to other areas 
within the area subject to the plan. The use of required internal landscape 
trees within and adjacent to parking areas and as an addition to required 
street trees, is encouraged.  
2. Street Trees. The Street Tree standards of 6.3.6 shall be adjacent to 
arterials, collectors, local streets, and interstates subject to City Forestry’s 
standards for street trees and the Street Design Standards of Section 4.3, 
at the rate of one tree per sixty (60) feet of street frontage.  Street trees 
shall be in the adjacent public right-of-way or seventy-five (75) feet of 
the property line adjacent to a street right-of-way if circumstances unique 
to the site prevent the placement of street trees within the right-of-way. 
 
d. Tree Reduction and Relief of Requirements Allowance. Up to a twenty (20) 
percent reduction of the required number of internal and/or street trees may be 
granted by the Director based on specific site conditions and to best meet the 
development’s collective appearance. If a tree reduction is approved: 
 
1. A per tree monetary donation shall be made by the applicant to the City, 
for every tree reduced, to be deposited in a specific fund for use in 
purchasing and installing trees for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway and 
other City lands, including but not limited to, open spaces, park areas and 
rights-of-way. 
2. The City Forester shall determine the monetary donation per tree yearly on 
January 1st of each year, based on the average wholesale and installation 
cost of trees from three regional wholesale nurseries. This fee shall be in 
effect for the calendar year and be available to the public. 
3. A per tree monetary reduction can count toward the point system as a new 
tree. 
 

 
 
Page 3 of 4 
e. 
Review Criteria. In reviewing, providing comments, and making decisions 
on a proposed common landscape plan, the Director shall use the following 
criteria: 
 
1. The proposed alternative design achieves the intent of the subject design 
standard to the same or better degree than the subject standard; achieves 
the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne to the same or better degree than 
the subject standard; and results in benefits to the community that are 
equivalent to or better than compliance with the subject standard; or 
2. The required landscaping on each lot is not practical because of location, 
topographic, or site constraints while the alternative design can 
accommodate site-specific issues. 
3. The plan represents a logical phasing plan for the development.   
4. Adequate utilities exist to serve the landscape elements detailed in the 
plan. 
5. The plan shall not place an undue fiscal burden on the City or other public 
entities.   
6. An entity has been identified for maintenance of common landscape plan 
elements and any necessary agreements can be executed within sixty (60) 
days of approval. 
7. Incorporates an area of at least eighty (80) contiguous acres.  For the 
purposes of area calculation, intervening rights-of-way shall be included in 
the measurement.   
8. The plan is prepared by a licensed landscape architect. 
 
f. Amendments and Alterations. Amendments to the common landscape plan 
may only be initiated with the consent of all property owner(s) of all property 
subject to the common landscape plan or an agent or entity authorized by all 
property owner(s) through mutual agreement or a property owners’ 
association.  
g. Recording and Approval. Following approval of a Common Landscape Plan, 
the Director shall sign the plan and the applicant shall then record the plan on 
each affected lot or parcel in the office of the County Clerk and be responsible 
for any associated fees. The applicant shall provide a copy of the recorded 
plan to the Director prior to issuance of any building permits. 
h. Abandonment of Plan. In the event the applicant wishes to abandon the 
approved Common Landscape Plan, the Director must determine that 
abandonment of the plan is not detrimental to the plan area and that the 
applicant has taken reasonable and necessary steps to mitigate any non-
conforming site conditions on existing sites in the plan area. Requests for 
abandonment may be initiated by the Governing Body, the Director, by all 
property owner(s) of all property subject to the common landscape plan, or an 
agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) through mutual agreement 

 
 
Page 4 of 4 
or a property owner’s association. Upon Director determination that 
abandonment is acceptable, the Director shall sign any necessary releases to 
be filed with the office of the County Clerk.”  
 
Section 6. The aforementioned sections of the UDC are hereby amended as set out within 
this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this ordinance 
remain unchanged. 
 
Section 7. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
February 22, 2022 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, repealing UDC 5.4.7. UU – Urban Use Overlay District and 
creating UDC 5.6.9. UU – Urban Use Overlay District. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLN-22-00003 
PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, AICP, Planner II, Planning and Development 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
to repeal UDC 5.4.7. UU – Urban Use Overlay District and create UDC 5.6.9. UU – Urban Use Overlay 
District, noting the request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND AND DISCUSSION: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of Plan Cheyenne, and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The City of Cheyenne Plannning and Development Department drafted the Urban Use Overlay (UU) 
District in Spring of 2021 as part of on-going efforts to revitalize the West Edge District. The overlay was 
adopted into the Unified Development Code on June 28, 2021. Staff looked to mapping the UU Overlay 
District in late 2021 but noted that the regulations have been placed in the incorrect location in the UDC. 
Additionally, staff noted the verbiage in the Intent, Applicability, and Context limited the potential 
implementation of the UU Overlay District. This current effort remedies both situations. The proposed 
ordinance moves the UU Overlay District to 5.6.9. in the Special Purpose and Overlay Districts section of 
the UDC and proposes new verbiage in the Intent, Applicability, and Context of the District to allow more 
areas of the community to take advantage of the flexibility the UU Overlay District provides. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
 
The proposed amendments include the creation and amendments of the following sections taken from 
the drafted ordinance: 
 
Section 4.  That sub-section 5.4.7, Urban Use Overlay, is deleted in its entirety. 
 
Section 5. The subsection 5.6.9, Urban Use Overlay, is created to read as follows: 
 
a. “Intent. The UU overlay district is intended for Urban and Infill Development adjacent to the 
Central Business District of Downtown Cheyenne. The UU district is intended to transition and 
step down from the urban core and other urban areas of Cheyenne into the surrounding 
neighborhoods. 
b. Applicability. The UU overlay district is applicable to various lands envisioned for urban 
development adjacent to the Central Business District. A base zone district of P, MUB, or MUR 
must be in place in order for the UU overlay to apply. 
c. Context. This district is most appropriate in areas with existing or future urban development 
patterns. 
d. Lot and Building Standards. The following lot and building standards shall apply and replace the 
lot standards for the underlying district for all uses:

setbacks

side setbacks

lines fronting and adjacent to the 
Reed Avenue ROW

Buildings shall not have a pinnacle 
height that exceeds an elevation of 
6,150’ in the West Edge District.  
Total Lot Coverage (max.) 
90%, 100% if site provides a street 
furnishing or projection as listed in 
5.4.7.f.4. 
 
e. Permitted and Restricted Uses.  This section shall apply when the underlying zoning district is P, 
MUR or MUB.    
1. Permitted Uses: 
a. All uses allowed in the underlying zoning district are allowed as indicated in Table 
5-1, except as specifically prohibited or allowed in subsections 5.4.7.e.2 below. 
2. Prohibited Uses: 
The following Uses are prohibited in the UU overlay base zone districts:  
a. Impoundment Yards 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
b. Outdoor Sales 
c. Automobile Service Repair 
d. Automobile Service Station – Limited and General 
3. Allowed by Conditional Use in all UU overlay base zone districts: 
a. Moderate Industrial 
f. Site and Building Design Standards. The following development standards shall supplement 
those contained in this Code.  Wherever possible, and if not exempted, development shall 
comply with the standards of Article 5 and Article 6 of the UDC, and the standards of this 
section; however, in the event of a direct conflict the standards of this section shall govern. 
1. 
Parking, Lot Access and Circulation.  The minimum required Automobile Parking 
standards of UDC 6.2.4.a shall not apply to property within the UU district.  
(a.) Bicycle Parking. All new development or changes of use that require a Site Plan per 
section UDC 2.2.3.b. shall provide 2 bicycle parking spaces per 2,000 square feet of 
gross floor area.  
i. Required bicycle parking is permitted in the ROW so long as the parking 
does not interfere with the 5’ required ADA pedestrian zone. 
ii. Applicant will have the ability to provide cash-in-lieu for bicycle parking to 
the shared biking facilities fund. 
2. 
Commercial Building Design Standards. Development shall comply with the standards 
of UDC 6.7. with the following modifications:   
(a.) West Edge Area. Properties included in the UU overlay district that fall within the 
West Edge district, located between 24th Street and the Union Pacific Railroad from 
north to south and Carey Avenue to Missile Drive from east to west, shall 
incorporate the design standards of UDC 6.7; or, as alternative compliance, shall 
incorporate the design guidelines from Chapter 5 of the 2016 West Edge Area Wide 
Plan into any development that requires a Site Plan Review per UDC 2.2.3.b. 
(b.) Adaptive Reuse. Adaptive reuse is encouraged in the Urban Use Overlay District.  
Developments that incorporate an existing structure designated as a significant 
building in any overlying subarea plan, such as the 2016 West Edge Area Wide Plan, 
shall receive an exemption from the standards of UDC 6.7. Buildings that receive this 
exemption shall incorporate design guidelines from the applicable plan. 
(c.) Alternatives. Minor deviations from the standards may be evaluated by the Director 
to meet the intent of the small scale commercial and mixed-use design standards of 
the UDC through an alternative design to allow a site-specific plan that is 
qualitatively equal to or better than the strict application of a design standard 
specified in this code. This procedure is not intended as a substitute for a variance 
or administrative adjustment or a vehicle for relief from standards of this Code.  To 
grant an alternative design, the director shall find either:  
i. The proposed alternative design achieves the intent of the subject design 
standard to the same or better degree than the subject standard; achieves 
the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne or the applicable plan to the same or 
better degree than the subject standard; and results in benefits to the 
community that are equivalent to or better than compliance with the 
subject standard; or  
ii. The required improvement would be ineffective because of locational, 
topographic, or site constraints while the alternative design can 
accommodate site-specific issues.   
3. Street Trees. Shall comply with the standards of UDC 6.3.6. 
4. Street Furnishings and Projections into ROW. May be placed in the ROW so long as the 
furnishing does not interfere with the 5’ required ADA pedestrian zone. All street 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
furnishings and projections into the ROW shall be shown and approved by the Site Plan 
process per UDC 2.2.3.b. 
(a.) Street furnishings shall include benches, tables, chairs, decorative patio fencing, 
hosting stands, or other items permitted by the Director.  
(b.) Other projections into the ROW may include awnings, patios, or other projections 
permitted by the Director.” 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or is 
necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is consistent with the principles and policies within PlanCheyenne. 
Specifically, the proposed amendment addresses the following policies and principles: 
 
• 
Policy 1.1.B: Flexibility to Respond to Market Demands. 
The UU Overlay text amendment will provide flexibility with zoning regulations to promote 
infill development, adaptive reuse, and redevelopment of an important area in the City of 
Cheyenne. The flexibility should incentivize the market to consider developing in the West 
Edge. 
 
• 
Policy 1.4.A: Infill Opportunities 
The Urban Use Overlay text amendment provides the opportunity for infill development 
through regulations that promote flexibility, but also consider the character of the area in 
which they are located. 
 
• 
Policy 1.4.C: Downtown Revitalization and Mix of Uses 
The Urban Use Overlay can be applicable to the MUR, MUB, and P zone districts in or adjacent 
to downtown. As the Urban Use Overlay is applied to the West Edge it is anticipated to 
revitalize the area and further mix uses in the area. Staff anticipates the development of the 
West Edge will greatly benefit the downtown as a whole. 
 
• 
Policy 1.4.D: Infill and Revitalization Incentives 
The Urban Use overlay achieves several items that are mentioned in this policy; such as 
reducing parking minimums, providing flexible dimensional standards, promoting higher 
densities than currently permitted, and providing flexibility in design requirements for 
utilizing existing buildings. 
 
• 
Policy 2.3.A: Mix of Housing Types 
The Urban Use Overlay will lead to the West Edge being zoned MUB or MUR; each of which 
provide ample opportunities for a variety of housing. 
 
• 
Policy 3.2.B: Active Uses in the Street Level 
The Urban Use Overlay promotes active uses on the street level by permitting various street 
furnishings and projections into the Right of Way. 
 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
• 
Principle 4.3: The Cheyenne Area will build a safe, multimodal transportation system that 
consists of streets, sidewalks, bicycle facilities, and transit 
The Urban Use Overlay promotes bicycle infrastructure and pedestrian amenities through 
increased bicycle parking and an active pedestrian zone. 
 
• 
Policy 5.1.A: Historic Building Restoration 
The Urban Use Overlay promotes building reuse and restoration with flexible site design and 
building placement requirements for utilizing existing buildings.  
 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-602 (a):  
The governing body shall specify how regulations, restrictions and the 
district boundaries are to be determined, established, enforced, 
amended, supplemented or otherwise changed. 
Assessment:  
State statute states that the governing body shall specify how regulations 
regarding zoning are established and enforced.  The governing body 
possesses the authority to amend regulations and therefore can amend 
the Unified Development Code to reflect this text amendment. 
 
WYO 15-1-601 (a):  
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i) Regulate and restrict the: 
(D) Density of the population 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, 
industry, residence or other purposes. 
Assessment:  
This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code for 
the purposes of regulating the location of buildings and density of the 
population; thus, fulfilling the requirements of this statute.  
 
WYO 15-1-601(d):  
All regulations shall be made: 
(ii) With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character 
of the district and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
(iii) With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the 
most appropriate use of land throughout the city or town. 
Assessment: 
This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code for 
the purposes of encouraging redevelopment and infill development in 
the City of Cheyenne with respect to the existing character of the districts 
to which these regulations are applied. Furthermore, these regulations 
contain provisions for height regulations to preserve views of existing 
prominent structures. Regulations of this nature are allowable under 
state statue and may be modified by the Governing Body.    
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PLN-22-00003  
 
 
February 22, 2021 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment will promote general health, safety, and public welfare by reinvesting in the 
community and promoting walkable, mixed-use development. Furthermore, it is anticipated that 
this amendment will promote much needed housing development in the community.  
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
The Urban Use Overlay district provides an effective framework for redevelopment in near-downtown 
neighborhoods in Cheyenne, specifically in the West Edge District. However, staff feels this flexibility 
should also be available to more areas of the community. The proposed amendments allow for more 
utilization of the UU Overlay District and relocate the UU Overlay District to the correct location in the 
UDC. Staff is satisfied that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.  
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code to repeal UDC 5.4.7. 
UU – Urban Use Overlay District and create UDC 5.6.9. UU – Urban Use Overlay District, noting the 
request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
FEBRUARY 22, 2022 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-22-00030:  1720 Cleveland Ave. Zone Change  
 
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 2.18 Acres of land from P Public to CB 
Community Business 
LOCATION: 
South and east of Lincolnway, south and west of Cleveland Avenue 
OWNER: 
City of Cheyenne 
ZONING: 
P Public 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Open Space (but proposed to be Mixed-use Commercial) 
PURPOSE: 
To allow redevelopment of a commercial property 
PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, AICP, Planner II - Development  
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 1, Block 1, Wynn Addition to CB Community Business as shown in 
the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval, with the 
following condition: 
1. The Future Land Use Map for the area be amended to Mixed-use Commercial prior to (or 
concurrently with) the zone change application.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from P Public to CB Community Business for 
City-owned property. The land is developed with a fire station, which is proposed to be relocated. The 
purpose of the proposed zone change is to allow for future commercial redevelopment of the City-owned 
property once the fire station is relocated. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. PlanCheyenne also encourages infill development.   
 
The Future Land Use designation for this property is currently Public; however, it is anticipated 
that the designation for this area of the community will be changed to Mixed-use Commercial 
(MU-C). The MU-C designation recognizes that the location will likely redevelop by private 
enterprise once the fire station is relocated. Furthermore, the MU-C designation matches the 
Future Land Use designations to the north and west. 
 
Based on the policies and proposed future land use designation of the subject property, 
Community Business zoning is appropriate at this location. Community Business is an acceptable 
zone within the MU-C Future Land Use designation as it fulfills the commercial component of 
the designation, 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1, with conditions. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Several of the surrounding properties to the north are zoned Community Business and are 
developed with commercial uses. Furthermore, the properties to the west that are zoned 
Mixed-use Business Emphasis and are also developed with commercial uses. The surrounding 
properties to the north and west carry a Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis which anticipates 
predominantly commercial development. Lastly, the location adjacent to E. Lincolnway further 
reinforces the suitability of the site for commercial uses. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The Public zone is crafted for use by public entities, such as the City of Cheyenne. With the 
planned relocation of the fire station, it is anticipated the City will dispose of the subject 
property to a private entity for commercial development. The Community Business zone will 
afford the future developer a variety of opportunities for redevelopment compared to the 
current Public zone.  
   

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Community Business zoning typically anticipates larger suburban-style blocks, consistent with 
the current site at 2.18 acres. The existing pattern is compatible with the proposed zoning and 
staff is comfortable that the subject property will meet the requirements of Article 4 should 
future redevelopment require a subdivision process. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The Intent of the Community Business district is for retail, employment, service, and civic uses 
that may serve a variety neighborhoods. The Applicability of the district states that the CB 
district is appropriate in areas with moderate- or large-scale businesses can be anticipated. The 
location of the subject property along a major arterial, E. Lincolnway, and size of the lot, 2.18 
acres, yield conditions conducive to moderate-scale commercial businesses that will serve the 
surrounding neighborhood.  
 
The Community Business zone is required to meet the Large Scale Commercial Design Standards 
of 6.8. Staff is confident that the future development of the subject property will be able to 
meet these requirements.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The existing property has adequate area and site conditions to meet the standards of the 
Community Business district. There are no current applications for development of the subject 
property and staff is satisfied that the various uses permitted in the CB district will be able to 
meet the applicable standards at the subject property. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Staff presented the zoning map amendment to the Project Advisory Team on Thursday February 
10. No comments were received. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
 

UDC-22-00030 
 
 
February 22, 2022 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the proposed future land use designation for the subject property and 
surrounding development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant.  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from P to CB, contingent on the proposed 
future land use map amendment for the subject property to Mixed-use Commercial emphasis. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. (Current) Future Land Use Map 
3. Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS TO CB COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS 
FOR 
LAND 
LOCATED 
SOUTHEAST 
OF 
THE 
INTERSECTION OF COLLEGE DRIVE AND CHARLES STREET.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1,
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on March 7, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as: the north ½ of Tracts 7 & 8, Sunnyside Addition, 2nd Filing (less an 8,325sqft portion 
to State Highway 212), City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, is hereby changed to CB 
Community Business.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 1.9 acres of land from MUB Mixed-
use Business Emphasis to CB Community Business   
LOCATION:
Southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Charles Street
APPLICANT:
National Care Development LLC
AGENT:
Mark Stec 
OWNER:
Albert N. Simpson

MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Commercial
PURPOSE:

PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of the N½ of Tracts 7 and 8 (less 8,325sqft to St Hwy), Sunnyside 
Addition, 2nd Filing to CB Community Business as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that 
the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business to CB 
Community Business for 1.9 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. The property is anticipated to be 
developed in the future with a medical care facility and drive through facility on the southern portion of the 
site. The northern portion of the site has no proposed uses at this time.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development (although it is not the 
intent of the plan to change existing, stable neighborhoods).

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. Areas to the west 
and south are also designated Mixed-use Commercial. The areas to the north and east are 
designated Urban Residential. The intersection of College and Pershing is designated as an 
Activity Center. The subject property is roughly 1,0
at intersection. 
 
According to comprehensive plan the optimal City zone for the 
 future land 
use designation of Mixed-use Commercial is MUB. However, CB zoning provides for commercial 
development and is generally encouraged near activity centers.  
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, CB Community Business zoning is appropriate at 
this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
This property, and surrounding property to the east and west, are currently zoned with mixed-
use zoning designations. Although these nearby properties are currently developed with largely 
single-family uses, all of them could redevelop with commercial uses (compatible with the 
proposed CB zoning)
-use 
Commercial, which would also anticipate future commercial development.  
 
The property to the south is zoned with a high-density residential PUD. The PUD is already 
bordered on south by a CB-zoned area. This property to the south has the future land use 
designation of Mixed-use Commercial.  
 
The property to the north is zoned CB and is only partially developed with commercial uses.  
 
The future land use designation to the north and east is Urban Residential, however these 
properties are zoned with commercial and mixed-use zones, and the future land use map is 
 the location adjacent to a major thoroughfare 
and mixed-
acceptable.  
 
The proposed CB zoning would match existing zoning to the north and would be compatible 
with the development patterns in the vicinity. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is currently zoned MUB, which requires buildings to be brought closer to the street 
and have a pedestrian-oriented feel. Although Charles Street my provide some opportunity to 
be built with a pedestrian-centered development, College Drive is a major, WYDOT right-of-way 
with more limited opportunity to provide the streetscape anticipated by MUB zoning. The CB 

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
zone is intended for more automobile-centric development. The MUB zone allows a variety of 
commercial and residential uses, while the CB zone is more commercial focused. This location 
along a major thoroughfare is more likely to develop with  commercial-style uses. The 
specifically-proposed use associated with this zone change is allowed in both zone districts. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The existing block is within the anticipated size for regional commercial development associated 
with CB zoning. However, it is much larger than anticipated for mixed-use development. Mixed-
use development anticipates smaller blocks than regional commercial centers, so the proposed 
CB zoning is compliant with block sizes, while the current MUB zoning is not. The block is largely 
already developed and limited options are available to create meaningful street network 
connections. No additions to the street network are proposed.  
 
There are no existing approved accesses to the subject property, and it is unlikely WYDOT would 
allow another access off of College at this location (no matter the zoning). Development of the 
property would require access off of Charles or a shared access with an adjacent property.  
 
Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the area, but improvements may be required with 
development. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB zone is intended for larger commercial uses serving the community as a whole and is 
applicable in activity centers. Internal circulation between adjacent sites is encouraged. The CB 
zone applies commercial design standards. 
 
This property is adjacent to CB zoning (to the north), and the proposed zone change is 
effectively an expansion of an existing CB zone district which meets the intent and applicability 
of the CB zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposed in concert with this rezoning is a commercial business which 
would be allowed in either the current or proposed zone. The purpose of the zone change is to 
modify the required setbacks and building design standards. Should the proposed development 
not be executed after approval, there are also a number of commercial uses which would be 
eligible to be established at this location. 

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the CB 
zone at this location and concerns about access off of College. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
ifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 

UDC-22-00029
March 7, 2022
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MUB to CB. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Maps

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-08_TotalExtraction_75482853.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE CREATING A NEW SECTION 9.01.010, DEFINITIONS 
OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE AND WELFARE, OF THE CODE OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.” 
 
 
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Chapter 9.01, Title 9, Public Peace 
and Welfare, of the code of the City of Cheyenne is hereby created as set out in this ordinance. 
 
A. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this title is to set out definitions for misdemeanor 
offenses within the city.  This title compliments and does not supersede any Wyoming statutes.  
The city is authorized to prosecute violations of city ordinance and Wyoming statutes, pursuant to 
Wyo. Stat. § 5-6-101 and Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103(a)(xli) 
 
B. 
Definitions.  The terms defined in Title 9 Public Peace and Welfare shall be as 
follows:  
 
1. 
“Assault” shall be defined and interpreted consistent with Wyo. Stat. § 6-2-
501(a). 
 
2. 
“Batter” or “Battery” shall be defined and interpreted consistent with Wyo. 
Stat. § 6-2-501(d). 
 
3. 
“Harass” shall be defined and interpreted consistent with Wyo. Stat. § 6-2-
506(a)(ii). 
 
 
Section 2.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon passage and 
publication. 
 
 
FIRST READING: 
 
 
________________________________________________ 
 
SECOND READING: 
 
________________________________________________ 
 
THIRD AND FINAL READING: 
________________________________________________

Patrick Collins, Mayor

Section 3.  This ordinance establishes ward boundaries governing all municipal 
elections, including elections for governing body officers beginning with the 2022 primary and 
general elections for those taking office on the first Monday in January 2023. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon passage and publication.

Note 1: The freon removal fee must be paid in addition to disposal fee for all refrigerators, freezers, and 
air conditioners unless freon removal has been certified by a licensed repairman.  
Note 2: A “building,” for the purposes of Section 8.44.010 D of City Code, is defined as a non-residential 
premises using a water meter.  Buildings will be assessed at least a “Basic Rate Fee” for each collection 
point (i.e., container or set of containers) or charges based on the type and quantity of containers and 
frequency of pickups as outlined in this Solid Waste Fee Schedule.  Multiple collocated, non-residential 
buildings owned by a single property owner will be assessed at least a “Basic Rate Fee” equal to the 
number of collection points.  If 1.5 Cubic Yard or larger city dumpsters are used, the Basic Rate Fee is 
included in the fees for those containers.  Charges are normally included in water and sewer bills issued 
to property owners or agents of such premises by the Board of Public Utilities. 
  
Note 3: If material is left outside of containers (dumpsters) requiring manual pickup, the owner of the 
property may be assessed an additional cleanup fee for manpower and equipment time.  
 
Note 4: City residents must show identification (driver’s license, etc.) and certify that material is their 
personal household waste to receive reduced Transfer Station rates.   
 
Note 5: Electronic Components: Charges to commercial entities for recycling of electronic components 
are based on the City’s cost for recycling plus 10% for handling and processing. Contact the Solid Waste 
Division for current prices.  Electronic components (computers, televisions, cell phones, etc.) contain 
hazardous materials and must not be disposed of in the Landfill. Recycling of these components is free 
to city/county residents at the Transfer Station. 
 
Note 6: Tires larger than 20” will be charged by weight. 
 
Note 7: Residents must present documentation from a doctor to qualify for “Special Attention” services.  
Documentation must be updated on an annual basis to continue in the program. 
 
Note 8: Residential recycle containers will be equal to or larger than residential trash containers.  To 
qualify for the smaller 60-gallon recycle container, residents must also have a 60-gallon trash container.  
Residents may choose not to accept a recycle container; however, there will be no reduction in the 
residential automated collection fee. 
 
Note 9: Overweight or over-packed Roll-Off Containers will be charged double the disposal fee.  
Overloaded cardboard containers will be charged based on time required to empty the container. 
 
Note 10: Residential trash and recycle service may be suspended for full months only. Full month 
suspension dates are based on billing cycles as defined by the Board of Public Utilities. 
 
Note 11: The optional yard waste collection program may not be suspended, only discontinued. 
Residents who pay for the optional yard waste container receive pickup of up to one cubic yard of limbs, 
tree trimmings, hedge clippings and similar material per pickup.  Residents must cut items to less than 
four-foot lengths and contact the Sanitation Division to schedule pickup.  The Sanitation Division does 
not provide yard waste removal services to those who do not subscribe to the optional yard waste 
collection program.  
 
Note 12: Place automated trash, recycle and yard waste containers a minimum of two feet from any 
object, i.e., vehicles, trailers, fences, poles, trees, other containers, etc., and be sure the area in front of 
the container (toward the street) is clear of obstructions. A Container Placement Fee is charged each 
time a driver must exit the vehicle to move a container so it may be safely dumped without striking a 
nearby object. The fee will not apply if a container is blown over by the wind or other act of nature.   
 
Note 13:  These charges apply to customers who do not get a water bill.  Billing must be done manually 
by Solid Waste Staff.     
 
Note 14:  The Landfill Manager may request to inspect the origin of the soil and require testing to verify 
the soil is uncontaminated.  The customer will pay for any testing. 
 
Note 15:  The Public Works Director or Deputy Director may negotiate costs and terms for unusual 
requests to dispose of material at the Landfill.  Examples include but are not limited to:  material from 
large commercial demolition or remodeling projects; material such as dirt that may be needed by the 
City; and material from accidents or natural disasters.  Testing for hazardous materials may be required 
in these instances. 


--- END OF FILE ---

ORDINANCE NO.   
 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE INCREASING THE COMPENSATION FOR THE 
MAYOR OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, COMMENCING JANUARY 1, 
2025.” 
  
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. 
That Ordinance No. 3736 is repealed effective January 1, 2025. 
 
Section 2.  
Compensation. 
 
A. 
The Mayor of the City of Cheyenne shall receive compensation in the amount 
One-Hundred Ten Thousand Dollars ($110,000.00) per year commencing on January 1, 2025.  
 
B.  
This Ordinance shall be repealed if the City of Cheyenne changes its form of 
government to the city manager or commission form of government. 
 
Section 3.  
This Ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon publication, and the 
compensation herein shall be reviewed approximately every four years from the effective date 
hereof.

ORDINANCE NO.   
 
 
ENTITLED: “AN 
ORDINANCE 
INCREASING 
THE 
COMPENSATION 
FOR 
MEMBERS OF THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND 
PROVIDING AN EFFECTIVE DATE.” 
  
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. 
That Ordinance No. 3914 is repealed. 
 
Section 2.  
Compensation. 
 
A. 
Members of the City Council shall receive compensation in the amount Two 
Thousand Dollars ($2,000.00) a month.  Council Members may elect to decline any portion of 
compensation.  
B. 
In the event a City Council Member does not hold office for an entire month, he 
or she will be compensated on a per diem basis ($2000.00 divided by the number of days in the 
month, multiplied by the number of days in office during that month).  For the first day and the 
last day that the member holds office, he or she shall be entitled to one-half of the per diem rate. 
 
C.  
Cheyenne City Council Members will be compensated for non-local travel on 
City related business according to established City policy applicable to City employees. 
 
Section 3. 
This Ordinance will be in full force and effect on January 1, 2023, and 
apply to City Council Members whose terms commence in 2023 and thereafter.  This Ordinance 
will be reviewed approximately every four years from the effective date hereof.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-08_TotalExtraction_19461386.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
February 28, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Resolution to Uncommit General Fund Reserves  
 
 
On March 14, 2022, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance Committee 
for consideration to uncommit $329,630 in General Fund Reserves for the purchase of the former 
Hitching Post.   
On July 22, 2019, the Governing Body voted to approve the Land Purchase and Sale Agreement 
between the City of Cheyenne and Victory Real Estate Management LLC regarding Lot 1, Block 
1, P.A.S. Subdivision, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming for economic development 
purposes and committed the use of $329,630 in General Fund reserves to do so.   
In governmental accounting, any formal action of the Governing Body to commit the use of 
reserves requires the same formal action to remove the commitment.  Once this resolution is 
approved, this action will add 2.14 days of operating reserves to General Fund reserves. 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION UNCOMMITTING $329,630.00 IN GENERAL FUND 
RESERVES APPROVED TO BE USED FOR THE PURCHASE OF THE 
FORMER HITCHING POST, LOT 1, BLOCK 1, P.A.S. SUBDIVISION BY 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERING BODY ON JULY 22, 2019” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne is required to follow the Governmental Accounting 
Standards Board (GASB) Statement 54, Fund Balance Reporting and Governmental Fund Type 
Definitions; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne’s General Fund Reserves (Fund Balance) is separated 
into five categories, including 1) Non-spendable Fund Balance, 2) Restricted Fund Balance, 3) 
Committed Fund Balance, 4) Assigned Fund Balance and 5) Unassigned Fund Balance as defined 
in GASB Statement 54; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Committed Fund Balance category is defined as resources that are 
constrained to specific purposes by a formal action such as a resolution or ordinance of the 
Governing Body that also requires formal action at the same level or higher to remove the 
commitment; and 
 
WHEREAS, on July 22, 2019, the City of Cheyenne Governing Body approved the Land 
Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Victory Real Estate Management 
LLC for the purchase of real property described as a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, P.A.S. Subdivision, 
City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, for economic development purposes and 
committed the use of $329,630.00 of General Fund Reserves; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, as the purchase did not occur, the committed General Fund Reserves in the 
amount of $329,630.00 can now be uncommitted and become part of the City’s Unassigned Fund 
Balance to be used for other purposes; and 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that $329,630.00, previously committed on July 22, 2019, is now 
uncommitted from General Fund reserves. 
 
 
 
(THE REMAINDER OF THIS PAGE WAS INTENTIONALLY LEFT BLANK) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:38 am, Feb 28, 2022

PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick J. Collins, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
Attest: 
 
 
 
__________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
MARCH 7, 2022
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00025: Olivet Cemetery, 4th Filing (Expedited Review)
REQUEST:
A plat proposing platting cemetery plots.
LOCATION:
Northwest of the intersection of Pershing and Airport Parkway.
APPLICANT:
Casey Palma Steil Surveying Services
OWNER:
ZONING:
P Public
PURPOSE:
A plat of previously undivided land, and a replat of previously divided land, to 
create and restructure cemetery plots.
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend approval of Olivet Cemetery, 4th Filing, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body, with the following conditions: 
1. Update the map to reflect the status as a final plat approved by the governing body (instead of 
an administrative plat). 
2. 
 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a plat for approximately 1.5 acres of land to create cemetery plots. 
While the division of a cemetery into plots is not a typical subdivision, all other cemetery divisions of record 
have been approved by the Governing Body, including Lakeview, Bethel, IOOF, and the previous portions of 
Olivet located south of this proposed plat. The original Olivet division and the Jewish cemetery division were 
not readily available it the various record repositories.  
UDC; however, as no documents were able to be obtained indicating what (if any) division of Olivet north of 
the current 3rd Filing occurred, City Staff has determined this proposed plat requires Governing Body 
approval. 
The City has a cemetery layout for all cemeteries located in this area of the community, and the area north of 
third filing is blank. Various repositories were investigated by Staff and the applicant and, as of this writing, 
no previous plat has been found. Should the previous document be found, this plat may be withdrawn and 
the procedures guiding administrative plats may be followed. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the plat, the Commission must make 
the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d   a detailed analysis is below. NOTE  as a cemetery, not 
all review criteria are as strictly applicable to this application. 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
Future Land Use Designation Zoning

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne designates this area (and all surrounding 
property) as Public and Quasi-public. Cemeteries are given as an example quasi-public use.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
This cemetery layout provides reasonable access to all plots and is similar in nature to 
surrounding cemeteries. Urban design principles are largely not applicable to cemeteries, and 
the over-arching parcel (as an open space) 
   
 
The application complies with this review criterion, to the extent the criterion is applicable.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
The plat proposes dividing an area already owned for cemetery use. The over-arching parcel is 
part of an extra-large block (containing the airport), and connections could be provided through 
the over-arching property. A Greenway connection (linking Airport Parkway and Evans Avenue) 
is proposed in the vicinity, but outside the boundaries of the proposed plat.  
 
Community Facility Fees: 
Due to the public and open-space like nature of a cemetery, this plat is exempt from plat fees. 
 
 
Open Space: 
As a cemetery, this plat is largely entirely open space in nature. 
  
Required Improvements: 
While a form of land division, it is not anticipated that individual cemetery plots would require 
the same level of service as typical developable parcels. Utilities and access are available to the 
over-arching development. No re-alignment of utilities or easements are necessary. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, to the extent the criterion is applicable.  
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
This plat represents one phase of the cemetery  
. No specific 
phasing is proposed within this plat.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
No technical studies were requested or provided in conjunction with this platting action. 
Generally, the subject property with the proposed use will have a negligible impact on traffic 
and drainage.  
 
This review criterion is largely not applicable to this application. 
 
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat is to allow individual plots to be laid out in an over-arching cemetery parcel. 
The over-arching parcel meets the anticipated development pattern of the P zone.  
 
This review criterion is largely not applicable to this application. 
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
. The over-arching 
ownership and maintenance patterns are not anticipated to be modified with this platting 
action. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, to the extent the criterion is applicable. 
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
The surrounding area to the south and west is previously developed with cemetery plots. The 
areas to the north and east are largely vacant, but are anticipated to develop with cemetery 
plots in the future. This action maintains connections to the previously divided areas, and leaves 
reasonable access to the future phases.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, or 
general welfare.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The purposes behind the submittal and approval of a preliminary plat are largely not applicable 
to the division of cemetery plots. 
 
This review criterion is largely not applicable to this application. 
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Conditions are technical in nature, and will be required to be addressed prior to recordation.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As an expedited review, this Final Plat review criterion is covered by the equivalent Preliminary 
Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
Pershing Boulevard Complete Streets 
 
Cheyenne Area On-Street Bicycle Plan and Greenway Plan Update 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. All other plans are largely silent on 
the development of this parcel, anticipating the property to be utilized as a cemetery. The proposed plat 
is in conformance with this expectation. The Cheyenne Area On-Street Bicycle Plan and Greenway Plan 
Update suggested a Greenway connection in this area (connecting Evans and Airport Parkway) 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00025
March 7, 2022
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. The location of a future Greenway connection in this area (north of the proposed plat). 
2. Inability to find any documentation of a previous action dividing the northern portion of 
the property.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
This application meets all review criteria as filed. 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. 
s and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for 
approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of Olivet Cemetery, 4th Filing, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body.

1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Plat

Subject: UDC-21-00454 / Rocking A at Sweetgrass / Final Plat

Background:

2nd reading (or final consideration 3/28/2022 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their March 7, 2022 meeting and by a vote of 4-0, 
recommended the Governing Body approve this application.   
  
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached.

portion of the unplatted land to the east. 
LOCATION: 
South of Murray Road and East of Sweetgrass Drive.  
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning 
OWNER: 
Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC 
ZONING: 
Sweetgrass PUD (Ordinances 4232, 4286)

UDC-21-00451  
 
 
March 7, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend approval of Rocking A at Sweetgrass Preliminary Plat, subject to staff’s 
recommended conditions, noting that as conditioned, the project meets the review criteria for approval, 
advancing the Final Plat to the Governing Body. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for a 15.38-acre parcel of land to create 89 lots for 
Cluster Home (Attached) Development. There will be 12 open space lots with two of them being existing City 
owned Greenway. The land is currently vacant.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is zoned as part of the Sweetgrass PUD.  The Sweetgrass PUD is generally described as “a 
±2,349.5-acre community envisioned as a distinctive mixed-use master planned village, integrating 
retail, entertainment, lodging, employment opportunities, and variety of intimately scaled residential 
neighborhoods.”  The district generally allows a broad range of uses. The HDR (High Density Residential) 
land use category is located in close proximity to Laramie County Community College and planned retail 
/ commercial development along College Drive. These parcels are intended to provide for residential 
development to support the student and employee housing needs associated with the community 
college and surrounding community in the form of apartments, townhomes, and more affordable single 
family detached dwellings. To promote greater diversity, lower density development, such as that 
permitted within MDR and LDR districts, is permitted within HDR districts.
