SCOPE OF WORK 
 
 
Identify the Appraisal Problem 
➢ Client and Intended Users 
I was engaged to perform this assignment by Laramie County School District #1, which is 
the only intended user. 
 
➢ Intended Use 
This appraisal report shall be used for by the client for reference in estate work related to 
this property.  Any other use of this appraisal report is not authorized.   
 
➢ Effective Date of the Opinions and Conclusions 
The date of value is August 12, 2021. 
 
➢ Prior Services Performed 
I have not performed any services related to the subject property in at least the prior three 
years. 
 
➢ Type and Definition of Value 
The assignment is to estimate the market value of the Fee Simple Estate of the subject 
property.   
 
Market Value Definition 
Market value means the most probable price which a property should bring in a competitive 
and open market under all conditions requisite to a fair sale, the buyer and seller each acting 
prudently and knowledgeably, and assuming the price is not affected by undue stimulus.  
Implicit in this definition is the consummation of a sale as of a specified date and the 
passing of title from seller to buyer under conditions whereby: 
 
1. buyer and seller are typically motivated; 
2. both parties are well informed or well advised and acting in what they consider their 
own best interests; 
3. a reasonable time is allowed for exposure in the open market; 
4. payment is made in terms of cash in U.S. dollars or in terms of financial 
arrangements comparable thereto; and  
5. the price represents the normal consideration for the property sold unaffected by 
special or creative financing or sales concessions granted by anyone associated with 
the sale. 
 
This definition is from regulations published by federal regulatory agencies pursuant to Title XI of the 
Financial Institutions Reform, Recovery, and Enforcement Act (FIRREA) of 1989 between July 5, 1990, and 
August 24, 1990, by the Federal Reserve System (FRS), National Credit Union Administration (NCUA), 
Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation (FDIC), the Office of Thrift Supervision (OTS), and the Office of 
Comptroller of the Currency (OCC). This definition is also referenced in regulations jointly published by the 
OCC, OTS, FRS, and FDIC on June 7, 1994, and in the Interagency Appraisal and Evaluation Guidelines, 
dated October 27, 1994.  
 
 
 

SCOPE OF WORK, CONTINUED 
 
 
6 
Accuvalue, LLC 
Scope of Work 
 
This report is done with a limited scope of work.  The client requested an estimate of the 
Fee Simple Estate. The subject property was inspected by the appraiser. I also inspected 
information available from online, public sources. There are few market-rate sales of 
portions of right away or other strips of land for which data is available. I have used the 
most similar sales of which I know. I also used the “Across The Fence” method, which is 
common for land that cannot be used independently.

SPONSORSHIP AGREEMENT 
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND 
HOLLYFRONTIER CHEYENNE RENEWABLE DIESEL COMPANY, LLC 
 
1. 
Parties. The parties to this Agreement are the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a municipality 
organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
82001 (“City”), and HollyFrontier Cheyenne Renewable Diesel Company, LLC, a limited liability 
company duly organized under the laws of the State of Delaware, 300 Morrie Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming 82007 (“HollyFrontier”). 
 
2. 
Purpose of the Agreement.  The purpose of this Agreement is to designate HollyFrontier 
as the Presenting Sponsor of the 2022 Superday, the 2022 Superday Tour de Prairie, and the 2022 
Superday 5K Walk/Run, a Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events Summer Celebration 
which is annually hosted in June to kick off Recreation month in July.  This Agreement designates 
HollyFrontier as the Presenting Sponsor with no liability implied, expressed, or written at any 
event. 
 
3. 
Term.  The term of this Agreement will be from the date of the last signature affixed until 
June 30, 2022. 
 
4. 
Payment.  The City of Cheyenne will provide HollyFrontier an invoice to process 
payment directly to the vendor.  HollyFrontier agrees to pay the City the amount of Twenty-Five 
Thousand Dollars ($25,000.00) pursuant to the terms of this Agreement. 
 
5. 
City’s Responsibilities.  
 
 
A. 
As the Presenting Sponsor of the 2022 Superday, the 2022 Superday Tour de 
Prairie, and the 2022 Superday Family Fun 5K Walk/Run, in exchange for Twenty-Five Thousand 
Dollars ($25,000.00) of sponsorship revenue to the City, HollyFrontier will receive: 
 
i. 
Brand recognition as the presenting sponsor on all print (posters, brochures, 
banners, registration forms, and newspaper advertising), radio, social media (Facebook, 
www.CheyenneEvents.org, www.CheyenneRec.org), and television recognition as the 
Presenting Sponsor of the 2022 Superday, the 2022 Superday Tour de Prairie, and the 2022  
Superday Family 5K Walk/Run; 
 
ii. 
Two main entry locations with tents, tables, chairs, and banner recognizing 
HollyFrontier as the Presenting Sponsor; 
 
iii. 
Superday brochures for HollyFrontier employees to distribute to and engage 
with participants; 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 1:28 pm, Feb 23, 2022

2 
 
iv. 
Ten complimentary registrations for the Superday Tour de Prairie; and 
 
v. 
Twenty complimentary registrations for the Superday Family 5K 
Walk/Run. 
 
6. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
A. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to this 
Agreement, which are mutually agreed upon by the parties, will be incorporated by writing. 
 
 
B. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
Agreement is governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming 
shall have jurisdiction over this Agreement and parties, and the venue shall be in the First Judicial 
District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
 
C. 
Compliance with Laws.  HollyFrontier agrees to keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
Agreement. 
 
 
D. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this Agreement shall 
be in writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
 
 
E. 
Termination.  Either party may termination this Agreement for cause without 
liability in the event either party fails to perform. 
 
 
F. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and 
employees, does not waive its governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to 
the extent necessary for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written 
terms of the Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retain all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., 
and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement 
actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly construed, either against 
or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental or sovereign immunity shall be 
construed in favor of governmental immunity. 
 
 
G. 
Indemnification.  Each party to this Agreement shall assume the risk of any 
liability arising from its own conduct.  Neither party agrees to insure, defend, or indemnify the 
other. 
 
 
 

3 
 
 
H. 
Reporting.  The City of Cheyenne will provide HollyFrontier an estimated media 
value (paid and in-kind) and list of elements the vendor is spending/using to promote the event. 
 
 
I. 
Entirety of Agreement.  This Agreement represents the entire and integrated 
Agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 
agreements, whether written or oral.  
 
 
In witness thereof, the parties to this Agreement, through their duly authorized 
representatives, have executed this Agreement on the days and dates set out below and certify that 
they have read, understood and agreed to the terms and conditions of this Agreement.

MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT #_________________ 
BETWEEN CITY OF CHEYENNE, A MUNICIPAL CORPORATION  
And  
Arts Cheyenne, a 501(c)3 Non-Profit Organization 
THIS MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT made this ______ day of ___________, 2022, by and 
between Arts Cheyenne (ARTS CHEYENNE) and City of Cheyenne (the City), provides that: 
 
WHEREAS, Arts Cheyenne was formed as a non-profit entity in 2008 for the purpose of promoting 
Cheyenne as a vibrant arts community; and  
 
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 5954 designates Arts Cheyenne as the Local Arts agency for the City 
of Cheyenne; and 
 
WHEREAS, Arts Cheyenne plans to host events in the West Edge in the summer of 2022 to further 
community involvement and education on West Edge development; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City, has adopted plans in 2014, 2016, and 2018 stating their commitment to the 
redevelopment of the West Edge; and, 
 
WHEREAS, the City, as implementation steps to the adopted plans, has created the Urban Use 
Overlay in the Unified Development Code and plans to apply it to the West Edge to foster 
redevelopment; and, 
 
WHEREAS, the City has determined that the summer events hosted by Arts Cheyenne are 
important implementation steps of the West Edge vision and has determined that this project is 
deserving of public support. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the City and Arts Cheyenne in consideration of the mutual covenants and 
promises set forth herein hereby agree, covenant and represent as follows: 
 
Terms 
 
1. 
The City grants permission to Arts Cheyenne to paint, construct, or install public 
art in the City Right-of-Way. The public art may include, but is not limited to painted crosswalks, 
street murals, kinetic art, or QR Codes directing citizens to information on future development. 
 
2. 
Prior to the painting, construction, or installation of said public art, Arts Cheyenne 
shall obtain a Right-of-Way Encroachment Permit from the City Engineer. The application for the 
permit will contain a traffic control plan created by a licensed contractor that will specify any road 
closures or rerouting necessary to paint, construct, or install public art. 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:37 am, Mar 23, 2022

Page 2 of 3 
 
 
3. 
Arts Cheyenne shall only paint, construct, or install public art as approved by the 
City Engineer by the Engineer Plan Review Process. This will be a plan sheet at minimum showing 
proposed locations, layout, pavement markings, parking, and drive lanes.  
 
4. 
Arts Cheyenne and their artists shall complete all painting, construction, or 
installation of public arts in accordance with the current edition of the City of Cheyenne and Board 
of Public Utilities Construction Specifications and Standard Drawings.  
 
5. 
Arts Cheyenne may make any necessary repairs to the public art, including, but not 
limited to, restoring a damaged portion of the public art. Any said repairs may require an additional 
Right-of-Way Encroachment Permit or Engineering Plan Review. City is not responsible for any 
maintenance of public art installed by Arts Cheyenne. 
 
6. 
The City reserves the right to inspect public art installations during construction 
and at the time of completion. Should the City find the installations do not meet the approved plans 
or specifications, the City may remove said art at Arts Cheyenne’s expense.  
 
7. 
The City reserves the right to remove any public art installed in the right-of-way 
for maintenance or other actions the City deems necessary. Removal due to maintenance or 
aforementioned actions shall be at the City’s expense. The City will not be held responsible for 
replacement of public art. 
 
8. 
The Artist(s) warrants and represents that the renderings and the Public Art have 
never heretofore been published or copied and that the Artist(s) is the sole owner of all rights 
therein, including but not limited to the right to prevent the making and dissemination of copies, 
and the right to obtain statutory copyright extending to him/her the exclusive right to manufacture 
and sell the above items and copies for a full statutory term and renewal thereof. 
 
9. 
Arts Cheyenne shall not assign or attempt to assign, directly or indirectly, any of 
its rights under this Agreement or under any instrument referred to herein without the prior written 
consent of the City.  
 
10. 
Neither this Agreement nor any provision hereof may be changed, waived, 
discharged, or terminated orally and may only be modified or amended by an instrument in writing, 
signed by the parties hereto. 
 
11. 
All rights, powers, and remedies herein given the City are cumulative and not 
alternative, and are in addition to all other statutes or rules of law. Any forbearance or delay by the 
City in exercising the same shall not be deemed to be a waiver thereof and the exercise of any right 
or partial exercise thereof shall not preclude the further exercise thereof and the same shall 
continue in full force and effect until specifically waived by an instrument in writing executed by 
the City. All representations, warranties, covenants and the provisions hereof shall be binding upon 
Arts Cheyenne, its successors and assigns and inure to the benefit of the City, its successors and 
assigns. 
 

Page 3 of 3 
 
 
12. 
All notices shall be in writing and shall be deemed to have been sufficiently given 
or served when sent by certified mail when addressed to the parties at the addresses set forth below. 
Such addresses may be changed by notice to the other party given in the same manner. 
 
13. 
This Agreement and all covenants, agreements, representations and warranties 
made herein shall survive the execution of this Agreement and shall continue in full force and 
effect so long as the agreement is outstanding. 
 
14. 
If any provision of this Agreement is held invalid, such invalidity shall not affect 
other provisions of this Agreement which can be given effect without the invalid provisions and, 
to this end, the provisions of this Agreement are hereby declared severable. 
 
15. 
This Agreement expires two years from the date of signing by all parties or when 
public art ceases to exist due to natural causes or pavement maintenance operations.  Extensions 
may be granted upon written request if agreed upon by the City and Arts Cheyenne. 
 
16. 
City does not waive its governmental immunity, as provided by any applicable law 
including W.S. 1-39-101 et seq., by entering into this Agreement.  Further, City fully retains all 
immunities and defenses provided by law with regard to any action, whether in tort, contract or 
any other theory of law, based on this Agreement.  
 
17. 
Each Party to this Agreement shall assume the risk of any liability arising from its 
own conduct.  Neither Party agrees to insure, defend, or indemnify the other.   
 
AGREED TO AND ENTERED INTO BY THE UNDERSIGNED PARTIES THIS _____ Day of 
__________, 2022.   
 
APPROVED:  
 
City of Cheyenne, a Municipal Corporation

PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR 
(S E A L) 
 
ATTEST: 
 
__________________________________ 
KRISTINA F. JONES, CITY CLERK

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
March 23, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Waste 
Management of Colorado, Inc., for the Recycling Services Project 
______________________________________________ 
One (1) Responsive Proposal was submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #7-22, for the 
Recycling Services Project. 
 
The proposal was received from:  
1. Waste Management of Colorado, Inc.,  Fort Collins, Colorado. 
 
Upon review of the one (1) proposal by the evaluation panel (consisting of 3 members), the 
evaluation panel has recommended acceptance of the proposal submitted by Waste Management 
of Colorado, Inc., for a project award amount not-to-exceed $200,000.00.  This Agreement is based 
on actual quantities recycled, and is billed in accordance with the Fee Schedule attached as Exhibit 
A, to the Professional Services Agreement.  If future Contract renewals are executed after the 
original term of the Agreement ends, additional funds may need to be added as well. 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications based review and scoring of proposals.  Waste 
Management of Colorado, Inc., submitted the highest scoring proposal, therefore their sealed 
pricing envelope was opened & reviewed. 
 
Funding:   
023-14-1423-43-21301 (Solid Waste Fund / Professional Services) 
 
$200,000.00  
 

 
City of Cheyenne RFP #7-22 / Professional Services Agreement (PSA) 
Recycling Services

City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
RECYCLING SERVICES (RFP 7-22) 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and between 
the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (“City”), and 
WASTE MANAGEMENT OF COLORADO, INC., located at 5887 S. TAFT HILL ROAD, 
FORT COLLINS, COLORADO 80526 (“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become 
effective upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, 
in accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for Proposal (RFP) #7-22, incorporated 
herein by reference, and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, incorporated herein by 
reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by April 15, 2023. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
This is a unit price, not-to-exceed Agreement.  The City shall pay for actual services 
rendered.  In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, 
the Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed 
amount of Two Hundred Thousand Dollars ($200,000.00) as stated in Exhibit A, Fee 
Schedule. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:04 am, Mar 23, 2022

 
City of Cheyenne RFP #7-22 / Professional Services Agreement (PSA) 
Recycling Services 
Page 2 of 7 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy:

d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 

 
City of Cheyenne RFP #7-22 / Professional Services Agreement (PSA) 
Recycling Services 
Page 4 of 7 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to April 15, 2023.  The 
Agreement may be extended for Three (3) possible, One (1) Year extensions beyond that 
period.   The parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne RFP #7-22 / Professional Services Agreement (PSA) 
Recycling Services 
Page 5 of 7 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work.

20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of 
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental 
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health 
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health 
agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar 
infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence 
beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or 
conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance 
required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without 
liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be 
refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing 
that he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement, consisting of Seven (7) pages, RFP 7-22 consisting of Twenty (20) 
pages, the Consultant’s submitted proposal, consisting of Thirty-One pages (31), and 
Exhibit A, Fee Schedule, consisting of Eight (8) pages, contain the entire understanding 
of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or 
controlling this matter.

2. 
Purchase Price.  Seller agrees to sell to the Buyer and the Buyer agrees to 
purchase from Seller the Real Property for the appraised amount of One Million, One Hundred 
Eighty Thousand Dollars ($1,180,000.00) completed on October 11, 2021 by Daniel Holbrook of 
Accuvale, LLC with the appraisal review completed on October 14, 2021 by John R. Sherman, 
MAI. 
 
a. 
All improvements, structures, and fixtures situated on the Real Property 
will be conveyed in an “as is” condition without any representations or warranties 
relating to their physical condition and without ascribing any value to them. 
 
b. 
The Purchase Price shall be paid to Seller or, with written authorization, to 
Seller’s designee, in full at the time of closing, subject to pro-rations and adjustments; 
provided, however, that the Buyer’s duty to pay the Purchase Price at closing is subject to 
the requirement that the Seller completes an IRS form W-9 and otherwise cooperates 
with the Buyer in reporting the Purchase Price and the other terms and conditions of this 
Agreement to the Internal Revenue Service as required by law.

c. 
Prior to the date of closing, Seller shall secure the release and discharge of 
all mortgages, liens, assessments, leases, rental agreements, advertising sign leases, if 
any, and other claims which encumber the Real Property.  The Buyer shall have the right 
to cancel this Agreement in the event Seller is unable or otherwise fails, neglects, or 
refuses to convey good and marketable title to the Buyer in accordance with the terms 
and conditions of this Agreement. 
 
 
3. 
Payment of Taxes.  Prior to closing, the Seller shall pay, when due and payable, 
all property taxes and assessments asserted against the Real Property for the current year and for 
all prior years.  All such taxes for the current year shall be apportioned between the Parties on a 
pro rata basis as of the date of closing. 
 
 
4. 
Title. 
 
a. 
Prior to closing, the Buyer shall have the right to obtain a Title 
Commitment for the Real Property (the “Title Commitment”) at the Buyer’s sole cost and 
expense.  In the event the Title Commitment shows that the Seller is not vested with a 
good, marketable, and insurable fee simple title to the Real Property, or that the Real 
Property is subject to liens, encumbrances, taxes, or assessments which will not be 
discharged prior to closing, the Buyer shall have the right, in its sole and absolute 
discretion, to declare this Agreement null and void. 
 
b. 
Within ten (10) days following the receipt of the Title Commitment from 
the Buyer, Seller shall deliver to the Buyer the following: 
 
 
 
 
i. 
True, complete, and legible copies of all documents referred to in 
the Title Commitment to the extent that any such document has not been filed for 
record in the office of the Laramie County Clerk and ex-officio register of deeds; 
 
 
 
 
ii. 
True, complete, and legible copies of all documents that are known 
to Seller, that are not referred to in the Title Commitment and that may affect the 
marketability of title to the Real Property; 
 
 
 
 
iii. 
True and complete copies or originals of any lease or occupancy 
agreements for any portion of the Property; 
 
 
 
 
iv. 
All mortgages, notes, and loan documents for any mortgage which 
encumbers the Real Property on the date hereof, which mortgages, notes, and loan 
documents shall be discharged by the Seller prior to closing; and 
 
 
 
 
v. 
Copies of any and all materials relating to pending or present 
litigation involving the Real Property. 
 
c. 
The Buyer shall have twenty (20) business days from the date of receipt of 

 
Page 3 of 11 
the Title Commitment referred to in Section 4 in which to examine the same.  If the 
Buyer finds title to the Real Property to be other than as described in Section 4(a) hereof, 
the Buyer shall, no later than the expiration of said period of twenty (20) business days, 
notify Seller in writing specifying the defect or defects; provided, that if the Buyer fails to 
give Seller written notice of such defects before the expiration of said period of twenty 
(20) business days, the defects shown in the Title Commitment or in documents provided 
by Seller shall be deemed to be Permitted Exceptions and waived as title objections. The 
Buyer may raise as additional objections, however, any defect first shown by any title 
update between the date of the Title Commitment and the closing date by giving Seller 
written notice of any such defect within five (5) business days after the Buyer first has 
notice of same. If the Buyer has given Seller timely written notice of defects that render 
the title other than as required by this Agreement (“Defects”), and only in the event that 
the Defects were the result of Seller’s action or caused by Seller’s failure to act, Seller 
shall use their best efforts to cause such Defects to be cured within ten (10) days 
following receipt of such notice.  In the event the Seller fails to eliminate such Defects, 
the Buyer shall have the right to terminate this Agreement.  In the event the Seller creates 
any liens or encumbrances on the Real Property which are capable of being satisfied by 
the payment of money at the closing, Seller shall be obligated to use the Purchase Price to 
cure the Defects. 
 
d. 
The date for elimination of the Defects may be extended until closing. In 
the event Seller does not eliminate any Defect prior to or at closing, as the same may be 
extended under the preceding provision, the Buyer shall have the option of either: (i) 
closing and accepting the title “as is,” without reduction in the Purchase Price and 
without claim against Seller therefore, or (ii) cancelling this Agreement.  In the event of a 
Defect that was not caused by Seller’s action or caused by Seller’s failure to act, Seller’s 
only responsibility shall be to assign to the Buyer Seller’s Title Insurance Policy or 
Seller’s rights under Seller’s Title Insurance Policy, whichever is applicable, and 
cooperate with the Buyer in obtaining the assistance of Seller’s title insurer to remove the 
Defect. 
 
e. 
Seller shall not create any new exceptions to title and shall use their best 
efforts to prevent any other person or entity from creating new exceptions to title to the 
Real Property prior to closing.

Seller shall, at Seller’s sole cost and expense, obtain releases, at or 
prior to closing, for any and all liens or encumbrances affecting the Real Property 
caused by Seller’s action or caused by Seller’s failure to act as of the date of 
closing, pursuant to the terms of this Agreement.

all of Seller’s right, title, and interest to the Title Insurance Policy it obtained when it

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-08_TotalExtraction_40466424.pdf
City Contract # __________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
 
Landfill Tent Building  
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 
2022, between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Engineering & Consulting Alliance, located at 445 Parsley Blvd, Cheyenne, Wy 
82007 (“Consultant”). 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibits A, incorporated by 
reference and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed within eighteen (18) months 
from Notice to Proceed from the City. 
 
3. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances at the same time and in the 
same or similar locality. Upon notice to the Consultant and by mutual agreement between 
the parties, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not 
meeting such a standard. 
 
4. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
5. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug Free Work Place Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers and other consultants 
under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are 
required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance 
Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:20 pm, Mar 08, 2022

Page 2 of 6 
Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the 
responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this 
policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure 
their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or sub-consultants are found in 
violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
6. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result 
of death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations or orders to the extent caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term 
or provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by 
the Consultant, or its employees or sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  
The Consultant acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant 
to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
7. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or 
destruction of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of 
$1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Business Automobile Insurance 
Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with minimum limits for bodily 
injury and property damage of $1,000,000 per accident for the entire term of the 
Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the 
Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services 
under this Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ 
compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided 
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
a. Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the 

Page 3 of 6 
City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 
in effect at the time the Consultant begins the provision of services under this 
Agreement; and 
b. In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the 
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new 
certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect 
during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services under this 
Agreement; and 
c. Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years 
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this Agreement; 
and 
d. In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior 
to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this 
paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance written notification of 
the termination of said coverage and shall provide the City with an endorsement for 
an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period 
of time not less than five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has 
provided services under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and professional 
liability insurance policies. 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  
It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the 
city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that 
has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance 
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach 
of this Agreement. 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of 
insurance policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional 
insured.  The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates 
shall not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a 
minimum A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
8. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES  49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin or sex.

9. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of one 
hundred and fifty-nine thousand three hundred Dollars ($159,300.00) as stated in Exhibit 
A.  
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written 
statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and 
work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any 
payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that 
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this 
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to 
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of 
any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts 
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default remains 
uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
a. Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed the 
contract amount; or 
b. Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
This Agreement shall be for a period of eighteen months.  The parties may by mutual 
written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon 
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
a. Discontinue all services affected; and 

Page 5 of 6 
b. Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, reports, 
estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as may have been 
accumulated by the Consultant in performing this Agreement, whether completed 
or in process. 
c. In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant for 
accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. Such 
acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the 
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default 
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to 
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its 
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for 
the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retain all immunities and defenses available 
to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and 
all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement 
actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of governmental 
immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly 
construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental 
or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental immunity.  
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts 
and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to 
examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; 
and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age

and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color,

PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION

22. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT

Engineering & Consulting Alliance 
 
By: _____________________________________ 
         
Print Name: ______________________________ 
 
                                                                        Date: ___________________________________ 

EXHIBIT A 
 
Landfill Tent Building Project 
1441 Happy Jack Rd 
Cheyenne Wy 
 
 
I. 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION 
 
The Cheyenne City Landfill has a 31,500 square foot tent building that has sustained severe 
structural damage from ensuing windstorms. There is a total of sixteen trusses, spanning 150 
feet, that will need to be removed and recycled. The Engineering and Consulting Alliance (ECA) 
will create and oversee a work plan to remove the existing fabric and trusses, without damaging 
the existing concrete pad and push walls. The Engineer will design and manage the construction 
of a new cost-effective structure to be placed on the existing foundation. 
 
II. 
SCOPE OF WORK  
The project consists of the following tasks: 
Kickoff Meeting:  The meeting will be held at the Landfill, between ECA the and the City of 
Cheyenne representative(s). The meeting should last no longer than one hour and will cover the 
project approach, schedule, and discussion regarding the deconstruction of the existing 
structure and construction of a conex metal structure.  
Conceptual Design: Based on input from the Kick-Off Meeting, the Consultant sill develop a 
conceptual design for the conex tent and other supporting infrastructure. 
Design and Specifications: Based off the kickoff meeting, the Consultant will design and prepare 
construction documents for the deconstruction of the existing tent building. Consultant will also 
design a new cost effective that can withstand sustained winds of 120 mph for a minimum of 12 
hours.  
The following should be considered: 
➢ Consultant will collect all necessary data related to the project, including but not limited 
to, Surveying and Geotechnical work and any other work that would be necessary to 
produce the plans and specifications for the project. 
➢ Design will be completed in three phases: 60%, 90% and Final. The 60% and 90% will 
include periods for City review. Conference call, or meeting, will be held after each 
review period.  
➢ 60% Design Services: The services provided in the 60% design phase shall include the 
following: 
o Refinement of design scope based on results of Conceptual Design 

o Finalize traffic flow diagrams and general arrangement drawings 
o Finalize layout and site plans 
o Develop outline specifications 
o Develop opinion of probable cost based on the 60% design development that 
will have an estimate range of accuracy of +/- 30% of the future actual 
construction cost 
o Complete Design Development documents for City review 
o Design development review meeting or conference call 
➢ 90% Design Services: The services provided in 90% design phase shall include the 
following: 
o Develop construction design drawings and specifications 
o Incorporate previous comments and review changes 
o Identify design components and finalize details 
o Construction document review conference call or meeting 
o Incorporate City and County standards for bidding documents as appropriate  
o Develop opinion of probable cost based on the 90% design development that 
will have an estimate range accuracy of +/- 20% of the future actual 
construction cost 
o Compile design and bidding documents into single package representing project 
manual and submit for City review. 
➢ The Final design services will include the following: 
o Incorporate final comments and reviews on design document 
o Compile and submit Issues for Bid project manual. 
It is assumed that work for this project will be bid as one project. 
Bid Administration Support:  The Consultant will support the City in the contractor bidding of 
the project. This will include: 
➢ Participate in pre-bid meeting scheduled and organized by the City. 
➢ Answer contractor and City questions that arise during the bidding process 
➢ Review, at the City’s direction, the lowest responsive bidder bid package and make a 
recommendation of award if the contractor is deemed capable of performing the work. 
Construction Administration: Provide QC/QA and as-builts in electronic form for the project 
during the construction process.  
General Consideration(s): The new cost-effective structure should meet all of Laramie County’s 
and City building codes for a maintenance garage. The structure needs to be large enough to 
allow Landfill Heavy Machinery to easily enter and exit the building for repair operations.  
III. 
SCHEDULE 
Please provide a schedule with bid, indicating the timeframe in which this project can be completed. 
All work, to include construction of a new building shall be completed within eighteen (18) months 
from notice to proceed.  
 

 
IV. 
BUDGET 
Task 
Budget 
Bid Package Preparation 
$7,400.00 
Construction Cost Estimate 
$11,600.00 
Bid Administration 
$42,900.00 
Construction Administration  
$97,400.00 
Grand Total  
$159,300.00 
 
Attached to this exhibit is the budget breakdown and schedule for this project. The table above is a 
reflection of said schedule. Work will be completed on a time and material basis not to exceed the 
total submitted budget amount, without written approval from the City.  
V. 
PROPOSAL ASSUMPTIONS 
In addition to the assumptions listed throughout the proposal above, the following is assumed as 
part of this project scope of work, schedule and budget. 
1. Site geotechnical evaluation (including drilling and testing) services are part of this scope 
of work. 
2. Topographical surveying is included in this scope of work. 
3. Utility locating services are included in this scope of work. 
4. Draft and final review will be provided to the City electronically. No printed copies of the 
plans are included in this proposal. 
5. Review meetings will be held at the Landfill, consisting of City Representative(s) and by 
up to two of Consultants ream members near the dates listed on the submitted 
schedule. 
6. Bid administration support assumes preparation of two addendums and responses to 
four requests for information. 
7. Construction Administration includes the Quality Control and Quality Assurance. 
VI.      ATTACHMENTS 
• 
Landfill Tent Building Project – Breakdown and Schedule.

MEMORANDUM

Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE:

SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-14-22 for furnishing Two (2) New 2022 4WD 72” Front-
Mount Mowers, for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events 
Department- Parks Division 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-14-22 was solicited for furnishing Two (2) New 2022 4WD 72” Front-Mount Mowers, for 
the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events Department- Parks Division.   
 
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Bids were received from: 
1. LL Johnson Distributing Company.  Denver, CO. 
 
2. Heartland Kubota.  Sheridan, WY. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Sam Payne (Parks), and Jason Sanchez (CRE).  Their recommendation 
is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Heartland Kubota, for the purchase of Two (2) New 
2022 4WD 72” Front-Mount Mowers.  The total cost, less Trade Ins, is $74,350.00. 
 
Additional Background Information:   
The Governing Body approved Bid E-1-22 on August 23, 2021, to Heartland Kubota for the 
purchase of Two (2) New 2022 4WD 72” Front-Mount Mowers, for the City of Cheyenne, 
Community Recreation & Events Department- Parks Division.  The Kubota Tractor Corporation 
ceased production of the awarded model due to problems with the operation of the DPF (Diesel 
Particulate Filter) system on the F3990 series mowers.  As Heartland Kubota was unable to fulfill 
this order due to unavoidable problems with manufacturing, the City cancelled the awarded of Bid 
E-1-22, and resolicited under this Bid E-14-22.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
March 9, 2022  
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-17-22 for furnishing Aggregates on an annual basis, for 
the City of Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division 
______________________________________________ 
Three (3) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Three (3) bids were received from: 
 
1. Knife River d/b/a JTL Group Inc. (Cheyenne, WY) 
2. Simon Contractors (Cheyenne, WY) 
3. Croell Inc. (Cheyenne, WY) 
 
The bids were reviewed by Randy Hickman (Street & Alley), Craig LaVoy (Public Works), and 
Vicki Nemecek (Public Works).   
 
Their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Croell, Inc. for the purchase of 
Aggregates on an annual basis, for a total cost of $37,500.00 for 5,000 Tons. 
 
*Note* The Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) Requested 6,000 Tons of Aggregate under this bid 
also.  They will be awarding their portion ($45,000.00) separately. 
 
Funding:   
020-26-2616-42-32303 (1% Sales Tax 19-22 / Sand & Aggregates)
